3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.12103pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-19.75012pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hskip-20.00003pt\mathcal{P}_{1,3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2,3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{1,4}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2,4}\hskip-40.00006pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.64627pt\raise-51.81937pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.48613pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{l}\otimes\varphi_{l}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.8088pt\raise-55.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 22.85306pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 78.70616pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 123.28839pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 121.12045pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\qquad\mathcal{P}_{1,3+4}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{2,3+4}\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 146.39403pt\raise-52.18745pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{l}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 136.67963pt\raise-55.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.70262pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{1+2,3}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{1+2,4}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.06122pt\raise-67.85411pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{r}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.00359pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 78.70616pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 103.00359pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{1+2,3+4}\text{.}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(2.2.2)

2.

The map

\chi_{l}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathcal{P}:(a,\hat{a})\mapsto(a,\hat{a},0)

(2.2.3)

is a well-defined section along
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
, and the map

\chi_{r}:\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathcal{P}:(a,\hat{a})\mapsto(a,\hat{a},a\hat{a})

is a well-defined section along
\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
.
These restrict further to sections along the inclusion
\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
into one of the factors.
The transition functions w.r.t. these sections are the following. Suppose
a,a^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
m:=a^{\prime}-a\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
\hat{a}\in\mathbb{T}^{n}
. Then,

\chi_{l}(a,\hat{a})=\chi_{l}(a^{\prime},\hat{a})\cdot m\hat{a}\text{.}

(2.2.4)

If
a\in\mathbb{T}^{n}
and
\hat{a},\hat{a}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\hat{m}:=\hat{a}^{\prime}-\hat{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, then we have

\chi_{r}(a,\hat{a})=\chi_{r}(a,\hat{a}^{\prime})\cdot a\hat{m}\text{.}

Over
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
the sections
\chi_{l}
and
\chi_{r}
do
not
coincide, but differ by the
\mathbb{T}
-valued function
m:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}
,
m(a,\hat{a}):=a\hat{a}
, i.e.,
\chi_{r}=\chi_{l}\cdot m
.
They do coincide when pulled back to
\mathbb{Z}^{n}\times\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.

3.

We recall that the dual
\mathcal{P}^{\vee}
of a principal
\mathbb{T}
-bundle
\mathcal{P}
has the same total space but
\mathbb{T}
acting through inverses.
The map

\lambda:\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}_{2,1}^{\vee}:(a,\hat{a},t)\mapsto(\hat{a},a,a\hat{a}-t)

is a well-defined bundle isomorphism over the identity of
\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. It expresses that the Poincaré bundle is “skew-symmetric”.
We have
\lambda^{2}=\mathrm{id}
.
Moreover, the isomorphism
\lambda
exchanges
\chi_{l}
with
\chi_{r}
.

4.

The Poincaré bundle
\mathcal{P}
carries a canonical connection
\omega
, which descends from the 1-form
\tilde{\omega}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{T})
defined by
\tilde{\omega}:=-a\mathrm{d}\hat{a}+\mathrm{d}t
.
It is straightforward to see that the isomorphisms
\varphi_{l}
and
\varphi_{r}
are connection-preserving.
Moreover, the sections
\chi_{l}
and
\chi_{r}
have covariant derivatives

\chi_{l}^{*}\omega=-a\mathrm{d}\hat{a}\quad\text{ and }\quad\chi_{r}^{*}\omega=-a\mathrm{d}\hat{a}+\mathrm{d}(a\hat{a})=\hat{a}\mathrm{d}a\text{.}

(2.2.5)

In other words, they establish trivializations
\chi_{l}^{*}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathbf{I}_{-a\mathrm{d}\hat{a}}
over
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
and
\chi_{r}^{*}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathbf{I}_{\hat{a}\mathrm{d}a}
over
\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Remark 2.2.1
.

1.

The curvature of
\omega
is

\Omega:=\theta_{2}\dot{\wedge}\theta_{1}\in\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2n})\text{,}

where
\theta\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{n},\mathbb{R}^{n})
is the Maurer-Cartan form, and
\dot{\wedge}
denotes the wedge product of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
-valued forms using the standard inner product of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
on the values.

2.

Note that
\Omega_{1,3}+\Omega_{2,3}=\Omega_{1+2,3}
and
\Omega_{1,2}+\Omega_{1,3}=\Omega_{1,2+3}
, as well as
\Omega_{1,-2}=-\Omega_{1,2}=\Omega_{-1,2}
.

3.

An identity expressing the 2-form
\Omega
in terms of the Maurer-Cartan form on
\mathbb{T}
is

\Omega=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathrm{pr}}_{i+n}^{*}\theta\wedge{\mathrm{pr}}_{i}^{*}\theta\in\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2n})\text{.}

Since
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\mathbb{T}^{2n},\mathbb{Z})
is torsion free, this shows that the first Chern class of
\mathcal{P}
is

\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathrm{pr}}_{i+n}\cup{\mathrm{pr}}_{i}\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{2n},\mathbb{Z})

where
{\mathrm{pr}}_{i}:\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to\mathbb{T}
is regarded as a representative for
[\mathbb{T}^{2n},\mathbb{T}]=\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2n},\mathbb{Z})
.

The following discussion concerns the quite difficult equivariance properties of the Poincaré bundle and its connection. They will be used only in
section

5
, so that the reader may also continue with
sections

3
and
4
first.

We remark that the curvature form
\Omega
is
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
-invariant. However, the Poincaré bundle itself is
not
equivariant with respect to left or right multiplication of
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
on itself. We construct a connection-preserving isomorphism

R^{l}:\mathcal{P}_{1+3,2+4}\to\mathcal{P}_{3,4}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{\psi^{l}}

(2.2.6)

over
\mathbb{R}^{2n}\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}=\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
in the following way, where
\psi^{l}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2n}\times\mathbb{T}^{2n})
is given at a point
((x,\hat{x}),(a,\hat{a}))
by

\psi^{l}:=-x\mathrm{d}\hat{x}-x\mathrm{d}\hat{a}+\hat{x}\mathrm{d}a\text{.}

(2.2.7)

Indeed, we have, using
\varphi_{l}
and
\varphi_{r}
, an isomorphism

\mathcal{P}_{1+3,2+4}\cong\mathcal{P}_{1,2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{1,4}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3,2}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3,4}

and due to
eq.

2.2.2
it does not matter how in which order these are used. In the next step we use the sections
\chi_{l}
and
\chi_{r}
, and now we see that for the tensor factor
\mathcal{P}_{1,2}
over
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
it does matter how to trivialize it. Using
\chi_{l}
, we obtain

\mathcal{P}_{x,\hat{x}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{x,\hat{a}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{a,\hat{x}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{a,\hat{a}}\cong\mathbf{I}_{-x\mathrm{d}\hat{x}-x\mathrm{d}\hat{a}+\hat{x}\mathrm{d}a}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{a,\hat{a}}=\mathbf{I}_{\psi^{l}}|_{(x,\hat{x}),(a,\hat{a})}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{a,\hat{a}}\text{,}

all together resulting in the isomorphism
eq.

2.2.6
. Explicitly,

R^{l}_{(x,\hat{x}),(a,\hat{a})}(x+a,\hat{x}+\hat{a},t)=(a,\hat{a},t-a\hat{x})\text{.}

(2.2.8)

One might be tempted to assume that
R^{l}
establishes an action of
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
on
\mathcal{P}
covering the action of
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
on
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
, but this is not true. Instead, “acting twice” gives the formula

R^{l}_{(x_{1},\hat{x}_{1}),(a,\hat{a})}\circ R^{l}_{(x_{2},\hat{x}_{2}),(x_{1}+a,\hat{x}_{1}+\hat{a})}=R^{l}_{(x_{2}+x_{1},\hat{x}_{2}+\hat{x}_{1}),(a,\hat{a})}\cdot(x_{1}\hat{x}_{2})^{-1}

(2.2.9)

exhibiting an error term which should no be present when
R^{l}
were an action. If one uses
\chi_{r}
instead of
\chi_{l}
, one obtains an isomorphism

R^{r}:\mathcal{P}_{1+3,2+4}\to\mathbf{I}_{\psi^{r}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{3,4}

(2.2.10)

for

\psi^{r}:=\hat{x}\mathrm{d}x-x\mathrm{d}\hat{a}+\hat{x}\mathrm{d}a=\psi^{l}+\mathrm{d}(x\hat{x})\text{.}

Remark 2.2.2
.

Restricting to
\mathbb{Z}^{n}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\psi^{l}_{(m,\hat{m}),(a,\hat{a})}=\psi^{r}_{(m,\hat{m}),(a,\hat{a})}\quad\text{ and }\quad R^{l}_{(m,\hat{m}),(a,\hat{a})}=R^{r}_{(m,\hat{m}),(a,\hat{a})}

the latter corresponding to the automorphism of
\mathcal{P}
given by multiplication with the map

f_{m,\hat{m}}:\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to\mathbb{T}:(a,\hat{a})\mapsto m\hat{a}-\hat{m}a\text{.}

In particular, we obtain from
eq.

2.2.9

R^{l}_{(x+m,\hat{x}+\hat{m}),(a,\hat{a})}=R^{l}_{(x,\hat{x}),(m+a,\hat{m}+\hat{a})}\cdot f_{m,\hat{m}}\cdot(m\hat{x})\text{.}

(2.2.11)

2.3
Invariant metrics on principal bundles

We review the mathematical basis of Kaluza-Klein theory, summarized in the following
theorem

2.3.1
. Its relevance for T-duality has been recognized already in Buscher’s first paper
[
Bus87
]
on the subject. General discussions can be found in
[
Ble81
, §9.3]
,
[
CJ88
]
or
[
CJ83
, §3.4]
; the latter reference contains a complete proof.

Theorem 2.3.1
.

Suppose that
E
is a principal
H
-bundle over
X
. Then, there is a bijection

\left\{\;\begin{minipage}[71.55518pt]\begin{center}$H$-invariant Riemannian metrics $g$ on $E$\end{center}\end{minipage}\;\right\}\cong\left\{\;\begin{minipage}[255.5542pt]\begin{center}Triples $(\omega,g^{\prime},h)$ consisting of a connection $\omega$ on $E$, a Riemannian metric $g^{\prime}$ on $X$, and a smooth family $h_{x}$ of $\mathrm{Ad}$-invariant inner products on $\mathfrak{h}$ parameterized by $x\in X$\end{center}\end{minipage}\;\right\}

under which
g
corresponds to
(\omega,g^{\prime},h)
if and only if

g_{e}=\begin{pmatrix}g^{\prime}_{x}&0\\
0&h_{x}\\
\end{pmatrix}\text{,}

(2.3.1)

where
e\in E
sits in the fibre over
x\in X
, and the matrix on the right hand side refers to the decomposition
T_{e}E\cong T_{x}M\oplus\mathfrak{h}
induced by the connection
\omega
.

The connection
\omega
that appears on the right hand side will be called the
Kaluza-Klein connection
associated to the metric
g
.

Remark 2.3.2
.

theorem

2.3.1
is compatible with bundle isomorphisms:
for a bundle isomorphism
\varphi:E_{1}\to E_{2}
it is equivalent to be isometric for the metrics on
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
or to be connection-preserving w.r.t. the Kaluza-Klein connections
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
.

Remark 2.3.3
.

If the principal
H
-bundle is trivial,
E=\mathbf{I}=X\times H
, we identify connections
\omega
on
E
with
\mathfrak{h}
-valued 1-forms
A\in\Omega^{1}(X,\mathfrak{h})
as in
eq.

2.1
. Under this identification, a metric
g
corresponds to a triple
(A,g^{\prime},h)
under the bijection of
theorem

2.3.1
if and only if

g_{x,h}=\begin{pmatrix}g^{\prime}+h_{x}(\mathrm{Ad}_{h}^{-1}(A_{x}(-)),\mathrm{Ad}_{h}^{-1}(A_{x}(-)))&h_{x}(\mathrm{Ad}_{h}^{-1}(A_{x}(-)),-)\\
h_{x}(-,\mathrm{Ad}_{h}^{-1}(A_{x}(-)))&h_{x}(-,-)\\
\end{pmatrix}\text{,}

(2.3.2)

where now the decomposition on the right hand side refers to the equality
T_{(x,h)}E=T_{x}M\oplus\mathfrak{h}
induced by the direct product structure of
E
and the identification
T_{h}H\cong\mathfrak{h}
via left multiplication, see
[
CJ88
, p. 101]
.

Remark 2.3.4
.

In later applications of
theorem

2.3.1
,
H
will be a torus,
H=\mathbb{T}^{n}
. In particular,
H
is abelian. In this case, the
\mathrm{Ad}
-invariance in
theorem

2.3.1
is vacuous. Moreover,
eq.

2.3.2
reduces to

g_{x,h}=\begin{pmatrix}g^{\prime}_{x}+h_{x}(A_{x}(-),A_{x}(-))&h_{x}(A_{x}(-),-)\\
h_{x}(-,A_{x}(-))&h_{x}(-,-)\\
\end{pmatrix}\text{.}

In the literature, this is sometimes written as

g=g^{\prime}+A\odot A\text{,}

where, unfortunately,
h_{x}
is suppressed or assumed to be constant.

3
Buscher rules revisited

The Buscher rules formulate the local behaviour of metrics and B-fields under T-duality. A priori, they only apply to trivial torus bundles over Euclidean space, for instance, over a coordinate patch. We first review the Buscher rules for higher tori, give then a reformulation when the metrics are replaced by their Kaluza-Klein connections, and finally produce a completely coordinate-free reformulation. It is this latter formulation that we generalize in
section

4
.

3.1
Buscher rules for toroidal symmetries

We first recall the classical Buscher rules for a
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-symmetry. The case
n=1
has been treated by Buscher in
[
Bus87
]
. Rules for higher
n
are described, e.g., in
[
GPR94
]
, also see
[
Bou10
]
for a review.

The Buscher rules apply to a manifold
E=\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. On
E
we consider a Riemannian
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant metric
g
and a
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant 2-form
B
, the “B-field”.
With respect to the standard coordinates, we may identify
g
with a block matrix

g=\begin{pmatrix}g_{bas}&g_{mix}\\
g_{mix}^{\mathrm{tr}}&g_{fib}\\
\end{pmatrix}

with symmetric matrices
g_{bas}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{s})^{s\times s}
and
g_{fib}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{s})^{n\times n}
, and an arbitrary matrix
g_{mix}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{s})^{s\times n}
.
We also identity
B
with a block matrix

B=\begin{pmatrix}B_{bas}&B_{mix}\\
-B_{mix}^{\mathrm{tr}}&B_{fib}\\
\end{pmatrix}

where
B_{bas}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{s})^{s\times s}
and
B_{fib}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{s})^{n\times n}
are skew-symmetric and
B_{mix}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{s})^{s\times n}
is arbitrary. We make the assumption that
B_{fib}=0
, so that the B-field must not have components purely in fibre direction. Note that for
n=1
this is automatic. Pairs
(g,B)
with the condition
B_{fib}=0
will be called
Buscher pairs
.

To proceed, we form the “background” matrix

Q=\begin{pmatrix}Q_{bas}&Q_{mix}\\
Q_{mix}^{\prime}&Q_{fib}\\
\end{pmatrix}:=g+B\text{.}

The dualization process requires to form the “dual” matrix

\hat{Q}:=\begin{pmatrix}Q_{bas}-Q_{mix}Q_{fib}^{-1}Q^{\prime}_{mix}&Q_{mix}Q_{fib}^{-1}\\
-Q_{fib}^{-1}Q^{\prime}_{mix}&Q_{fib}^{-1}\\
\end{pmatrix}\text{.}

Dual metric and B-field are now obtained by taking the symmetric and anti-symmetric parts of
\hat{Q}
, respectively, i.e.

\hat{g}:=\frac{1}{2}(\hat{Q}+\hat{Q}^{\mathrm{tr}})\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{B}:=\frac{1}{2}(\hat{Q}-\hat{Q}^{\mathrm{tr}})\text{.}

A standard calculation shows that
(\hat{g},\hat{B})
is again a Buscher pair, and that the relation between the Buscher pairs
(g,B)
and
(\hat{g},\hat{B})
is described by the following equations:

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{bas}

\displaystyle=g_{bas}-g_{mix}g_{fib}^{-1}g_{mix}^{\mathrm{tr}}+B_{mix}g_{fib}^{-1}B_{mix}^{\mathrm{tr}}

(3.1.1)

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{mix}

\displaystyle=B_{mix}g_{fib}^{-1}

(3.1.2)

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{fib}

\displaystyle=g_{fib}^{-1}

(3.1.3)

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{bas}

\displaystyle=B_{bas}-B_{mix}g_{fib}^{-1}g_{mix}^{\mathrm{tr}}+g_{mix}g_{fib}^{-1}B_{mix}^{\mathrm{tr}}

(3.1.4)

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{mix}

\displaystyle=g_{mix}g_{fib}^{-1}

(3.1.5)

It is straightforward to see that these rules reduce in the case of
n=1
to the usual Buscher rules.
It is also straightforward to see that
\hat{\hat{Q}}=Q
, implying that the Buscher rules are symmetric in the data. For completeness, let us fix the following definition.

Definition 3.1.1
.

Two Buscher pairs
(g,B)
and
(\hat{g},\hat{B})

satisfy the Buscher rules
if
eqs.

3.1.1
,
3.1.2
,
3.1.3
,
3.1.4
and
3.1.5
are satisfied.

3.2
Buscher rules in terms of Kaluza-Klein connections

We consider again a metric
g
on
E=\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
, and consider
E
as a principal
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-bundle over
\mathbb{R}^{s}
. We apply
theorems

2.3.1
and
2.3.3
, to obtain a triple
(A,g^{\prime},h)
consisting of a Riemannian metric
g^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{R}^{s}
, a 1-form
A\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{s},\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and a family
h
of inner products on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
parameterized by
\mathbb{R}^{s}
. Now we consider
Buscher quadruples

(A,g^{\prime},h,B)
instead of Buscher pairs
(g,B)
. By
theorem

2.3.1
there is a bijection between Buscher quadruples and Buscher pairs.

The expression for the metric
g
given in
remark

2.3.4
now reads

g=\begin{pmatrix}g^{\prime}+A^{\mathrm{tr}}hA&A^{\mathrm{tr}}h\\
hA&h\\
\end{pmatrix}\text{.}

In other words, we have

\displaystyle g_{bas}=g^{\prime}+A^{\mathrm{tr}}hA\quad\text{, }\quad g_{mix}=A^{\mathrm{tr}}h\quad\text{ and }\quad g_{fib}=h\text{.}

We employ the same procedure on the dual side, getting

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{bas}=\hat{g}^{\prime}+\hat{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}\hat{h}\hat{A}\quad\text{, }\quad\hat{g}_{mix}=\hat{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}\hat{h}\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{g}_{fib}=\hat{h}\text{.}

The Buscher rules now attain the following simple form:

\displaystyle\hat{g}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=g^{\prime}

(3.2.1)

\displaystyle\hat{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}

\displaystyle=B_{mix}

(3.2.2)

\displaystyle\hat{h}

\displaystyle=h^{-1}

(3.2.3)

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{bas}

\displaystyle=B_{bas}-B_{mix}A+(B_{mix}A)^{\mathrm{tr}}

(3.2.4)

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{mix}

\displaystyle=A^{\mathrm{tr}}

(3.2.5)

Again for completeness, we fix the following definition and result.

Definition 3.2.1
.

Two Buscher quadruples
(A,g^{\prime},h,B)
and
(\hat{A},\hat{g}^{\prime},\hat{h},\hat{B})

satisfy the Buscher rules
, if the
eqs.

3.2.1
,
3.2.2
,
3.2.3
,
3.2.4
and
3.2.5
are satisfied.

Lemma 3.2.2
.

Under the bijection between Buscher pairs and Buscher quadruples, the Buscher rules of
definitions

3.1.1
and
3.2.1
are equivalent.

3.3
Buscher rules in terms of Poincaré forms

Next we want to give a coordinate-independent description of the Buscher rules of
definition

3.2.1
, which will again make them simpler. Let
\omega,\hat{\omega}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{n},\mathbb{R}^{n})
be the Kaluza-Klein connections on
E=\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
corresponding to
A
and
\hat{A}
, respectively, i.e.,
\omega:=A_{1}+\theta_{2}
and
\hat{\omega}:=\hat{A}_{1}+\theta_{2}
. Here, the indices refer to the pullback along the projections to the two factors, as explained in
section

2.2
. We introduce the 2-form

\rho:=\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\omega}\dot{\wedge}{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\omega\in\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{2n})\text{,}

where the symbol
\dot{\wedge}
means that the standard scalar product on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is used in the values the forms.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

Buscher quadruples
(g^{\prime},A,h,B)
and
(\hat{g}^{\prime},\hat{A},\hat{h},\hat{B})
satisfy the Buscher rules of
definition

3.2.1
if and only if the following conditions are satisfied:

1.

\hat{g}^{\prime}=g^{\prime}

2.

\hat{h}=h^{-1}

3.

\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{B}-{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}B={\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\Omega-\rho
.

(a) and (b) are
eqs.

3.2.1
and
3.2.3
. We have

-\rho_{x,t,\hat{t}}=\omega_{x,t}\dot{\wedge}\hat{\omega}_{x,\hat{t}}=(A_{x}+\theta_{t}^{\mathbb{T}^{n}})\dot{\wedge}(\hat{A}_{x}+\theta_{\hat{t}}^{\mathbb{T}^{n}})=A_{x}\dot{\wedge}\hat{A}_{x}+A_{x}\dot{\wedge}\theta_{\hat{t}}^{\mathbb{T}^{n}}+\theta_{t}^{\mathbb{T}^{n}}\dot{\wedge}\hat{A}_{x}+\theta_{t}^{\mathbb{T}^{n}}\dot{\wedge}\theta_{\hat{t}}^{\mathbb{T}^{n}}\text{.}

We change to coordinates w.r.t.
\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
, which we label by
i
,
\mu
, and
\hat{\mu}
.
Then, we obtain

-\rho(e_{i},e_{j})=A_{i}\hat{A}_{j}-A_{j}\hat{A}_{i}\quad\text{, }\quad-\rho(e_{i},e_{\mu})=-\hat{A}_{\mu i}\quad\text{, }\quad-\rho(e_{i},e_{\hat{\mu}})=A_{\hat{\mu}i}\quad\text{ and }\quad-\rho(e_{\mu},e_{\hat{\nu}})=\delta_{\mu\hat{\nu}}

Note that

(B_{bas})_{ij}=({\mathrm{pr}}^{*}B)(e_{i},e_{j})\quad\text{, }\quad(B_{mix})_{i\mu}=({\mathrm{pr}}^{*}B)(e_{i},e_{\mu})\quad\text{ and }\quad(B_{fib})_{\mu\nu}=({\mathrm{pr}}^{*}B)(e_{\mu},e_{\nu})

and similarly

(\hat{B}_{bas})_{ij}=(\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{B})(e_{i},e_{j})\quad\text{, }\quad(\hat{B}_{mix})_{i\hat{\mu}}=(\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{B})(e_{i},e_{\hat{\mu}})\quad\text{ and }\quad(\hat{B}_{fib})_{\hat{\mu}\hat{\nu}}=(\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{B})(e_{\hat{\mu}},e_{\hat{\nu}})\text{,}

and all other components vanish.
Further, we have

({\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\Omega)(e_{\mu},e_{\hat{\nu}})=-\delta_{\mu\hat{\nu}}\text{,}

with again all other components vanishing.
Thus, (c) is equivalent to the following set of equations:

\displaystyle(\hat{B}_{bas})_{ij}-(B_{bas})_{ij}

\displaystyle=A_{i}\hat{A}_{j}-A_{j}\hat{A}_{i}

\displaystyle(B_{mix})_{i\mu}

\displaystyle=\hat{A}_{\mu i}

\displaystyle(\hat{B}_{mix})_{i\hat{\mu}}

\displaystyle=A_{\hat{\mu}i}

The second and third equation are
eqs.

3.2.2
and
3.2.5
. The first equation, using second and third, is equivalent to

\hat{B}_{bas}-B_{bas}=(B_{mix}A)^{\mathrm{tr}}-B_{mix}A

and this is precisely
eq.

3.2.4
.
∎

A straightforward computation using
item

3
shows the following.

Lemma 3.3.2
.

Suppose
(A,g^{\prime},h,B)
and
(\hat{A},\hat{g}^{\prime},\hat{h},\hat{B})
are Buscher quadruples satisfying the Buscher rules of
definition

3.2.1
. Then, we have

\displaystyle B_{1,2+3}

\displaystyle=B_{1,2}+\hat{A}_{1}\wedge\theta_{3}

(3.3.1)

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{1,2+3}

\displaystyle=\hat{B}_{1,2}+A_{1}\wedge\theta_{3}

(3.3.2)

over
\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. In particular,
B
and
\hat{B}
are
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant.

4
Geometric T-duality

In this section we give the central definitions of this article: we introduce geometric T-backgrounds (
definition

4.1.1
) and geometric T-duality correspondences between them (
definition

4.1.9
). We deduce a number of first consequences; in particular, we relate geometric T-duality to T-duality with H-flux and to topological T-duality.

4.1
Basic definitions

Definition 4.1.1
.

A
geometric T-background
over a smooth manifold
X
is a triple
(E,g,\mathcal{G})
consisting of a principal
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-bundle
E
over
X
, a
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant Riemannian metric
g
on
E
, and a bundle gerbe
\mathcal{G}
over
E
with connection. Two geometric T-backgrounds
(E_{1},g_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
and
(E_{2},g_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
over
X
are equivalent, if there exists a bundle isomorphism
f:E_{1}\to E_{2}
that is isometric with respect to the metrics
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
, and a connection-preserving bundle gerbe isomorphism
\mathcal{G}_{1}\cong f^{*}\mathcal{G}_{2}
. The set of equivalence classes of geometric T-backgrounds over
X
is denoted by
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{BG}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.

As every bundle gerbe with connection has a curvature 3-form, every geometric T-background carries a 3-form
H\in\Omega^{3}(E)
, the
H-flux
. Note that
H
is closed, but in general not exact. The H-fluxes of equivalent geometric T-backgrounds satisfy
H_{1}=f^{*}H_{2}
.

If

(E,g,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
are geometric T-backgrounds over the same manifold
X
, then the principal
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
-bundle
E\times_{X}\hat{E}
is called the
correspondence space
. It fits into an important commutative diagram:

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.97916pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 30.97916pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E\times_{X}\hat{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.71892pt\raise-9.25pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.09512pt\raise-27.9362pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.66016pt\raise-10.67361pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.97916pt\raise-27.25122pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-6.97916pt\raise-31.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.20708pt\raise-52.497pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.00243pt\raise-58.45987pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 46.53714pt\raise-31.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 92.09512pt\raise-31.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hat{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.57481pt\raise-53.52415pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{p}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.07185pt\raise-58.33478pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-63.25pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 42.00243pt\raise-63.25pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Let
\omega\in\Omega^{1}(E,\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\hat{\omega}\in\Omega^{1}(\hat{E},\mathbb{R}^{n})
be the Kaluza-Klein connections of the metrics
g
and
\hat{g}
, respectively, under
theorem

2.3.1
. Then, we consider the 2-form

\rho_{g,\hat{g}}:=\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\omega}\dot{\wedge}{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\omega\in\Omega^{2}(E\times_{X}\hat{E})\text{,}

(4.1.1)

where
\dot{\wedge}
denotes the wedge product of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
-valued forms w.r.t. the standard inner product. Since
\omega
and
\hat{\omega}
are
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant (they are connections on a principal bundle with abelian structure group), the 2-form
\rho_{g,\hat{g}}
is
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
-invariant. We remark that the 2-form
\rho_{g,\hat{g}}
also appeared in
[
Hor99
,
BEM04b
]
.

Definition 4.1.2
.

A
geometric correspondence
over
X
consists of two geometric T-backgrounds

(E,g,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
over
X
, and a connection-preserving bundle gerbe isomorphism

\mathcal{D}:{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{G}\to\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\rho_{g,\hat{g}}}\text{.}

Remark 4.1.3
.

We shall explore some consequences of the isomorphism
\mathcal{D}
in a geometric correspondence. For this, we will denote by
F,\hat{F}\in\Omega^{2}(X)
the curvatures of the connections
\omega
and
\hat{\omega}
, respectively.

1.

Since the curvatures of isomorphic bundle gerbes with connection coincide, we have

{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}H-\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{H}=\mathrm{d}\rho_{g,\hat{g}}\text{,}

(4.1.2)

which is a condition in the context of T-duality with H-flux, see
[
BEM04b
, Eq. 1.12]
and
definition

4.4.2
.
From
eq.

4.1.2
and the definition of
\rho_{g,\hat{g}}
one can deduce the equivariance rule

R^{*}H=e^{*}H+e^{*}p^{*}\hat{F}\,\dot{\wedge}\,h^{*}\theta

(4.1.3)

on
E\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
, where
R
is the principal action,
e
the projection to
E
, and
h
the projection to
\mathbb{T}^{n}
.
Similarly, on the dual side we obtain

R^{*}\hat{H}=\hat{e}^{*}\hat{H}+\hat{e}^{*}\hat{p}^{*}F\wedge h^{*}\theta

(4.1.4)

on
\hat{E}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. In particular, these formulas show that
H
and
\hat{H}
are
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant.

2.

We consider the 3-forms

\tilde{K}:=\omega\dot{\wedge}\,p^{*}\hat{F}-H\in\Omega^{3}(E)\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{K}:=\hat{\omega}\dot{\wedge}\,\hat{p}^{*}F-\hat{H}\in\Omega^{3}(\hat{E})

Using
eqs.

4.1.3
and
4.1.4
one can show that
R^{*}\tilde{K}=e^{*}\tilde{K}
and
R^{*}\hat{K}=\hat{e}^{*}\hat{K}
, so that these forms descend to
X
. In fact,
\tilde{K}
and
\hat{K}
both descend to the
same
3-form
K\in\Omega^{3}(X)
, i.e.,
p^{*}K=\tilde{K}
and
\hat{p}^{*}K=\hat{K}
. To see this, it suffices to note that the pullbacks of
\tilde{K}
and
\hat{K}
to the correspondence space coincide, which again can be checked using
eqs.

4.1.2
and
4.1.1
.
Summarizing, every geometric correspondence determines a 3-form
K\in\Omega^{3}(X)

such that

p^{*}K=\omega\dot{\wedge}\,p^{*}\hat{F}-H\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{p}^{*}K=\hat{\omega}\dot{\wedge}\,\hat{p}^{*}F-\hat{H}\text{.}

Note that
\mathrm{d}K=F\dot{\wedge}\hat{F}
.

Remark 4.1.4
.

Geometric correspondence is a symmetric relation on the set
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{BG}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.
If
\mathcal{D}
is a correspondence from
(E,g,\mathcal{G})
to
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
, then we construct a correspondence from
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
to
(E,g,\mathcal{G})
as follows. Let
s:\hat{E}\times_{X}E\to E\times_{X}\hat{E}
denote the swap map. Then, we consider

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 59.68912pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-59.68912pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}=\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{s^{*}\rho_{g,\hat{g}}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{-s^{*}\rho_{g,\hat{g}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.10397pt\raise 6.53668pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.70334pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{s^{*}\mathcal{D}^{-1}\otimes\mathrm{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 109.6892pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 109.6892pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{-s^{*}\rho_{g,\hat{g}}}\text{.}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Since
-s^{*}\rho_{g,\hat{g}}=\rho_{\hat{g},g}
, this is again a geometric correspondence.

Definition 4.1.5
.

Two geometric correspondences over
X
,
((E,g,\mathcal{G}),(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}}),\mathcal{D})
and
((E^{\prime},g^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}),(\hat{E}^{\prime},\hat{g}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}),\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
, are considered to be equivalent, if there exist isometric bundle isomorphisms
f:E\to E^{\prime}
and
\hat{f}:\hat{E}\to\hat{E}^{\prime}
, connection-preserving bundle gerbe isomorphisms
\mathcal{A}:\mathcal{G}\to f^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
\hat{\mathcal{A}}:\hat{\mathcal{G}}\to\hat{f}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}
, and a connection-preserving 2-isomorphism

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 91.51053pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.6251pt\raise-15.93501pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}^{\prime*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.19516pt\raise-69.78564pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{F^{*}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.72232pt\raise-63.88785pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 72.72232pt\raise-63.88785pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F^{*}\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{\prime*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}\otimes F^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\rho_{g^{\prime},\hat{g}^{\prime}}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

where
F:=f\times\hat{f}:E\times_{X}\hat{E}\to E^{\prime}\times_{X}\hat{E}^{\prime}
. The set of equivalence classes of geometric correspondences over
X
is denoted by
\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.

Remark 4.1.6
.

In above definition we have implicitly used that
F^{*}\rho_{g^{\prime},\hat{g}^{\prime}}=\rho_{g_{1},\hat{g}_{1}}
, which follows from the fact that
f
and
\hat{f}
are connection-preserving, which in turn follows from the assumption that
f
and
\hat{f}
are isometric (
remark

2.3.2
).

Remark 4.1.7
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a bundle gerbe with connection over
X
. Then, we may send

((E,g,\mathcal{G}),(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}}),\mathcal{D})\mapsto((E,g,\mathcal{G}\otimes p^{*}\mathcal{H}),(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}}\otimes\hat{p}^{*}\mathcal{H}),\mathcal{D}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{H}})\text{.}

This gives a well-defined action of the group of isomorphism classes of bundle gerbes with connection on the set of equivalence classes of geometric correspondences,

\mathrm{Grb}^{\nabla}(X)\times\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)\to\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)\text{.}

Remark 4.1.8
.

It is straightforward to see that equivalent geometric correspondences determine the same 3-form
K
. The action of
remark

4.1.7
shifts this 3-form by
\mathrm{curv}(\mathcal{H})
.

Definition 4.1.9
.

A geometric correspondence
\mathcal{D}
between two geometric T-backgrounds

(E,g,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
over
X
is called
geometric T-duality correspondence
if the following conditions hold:

1.

The Riemannian metrics
g^{\prime}
and
\hat{g}^{\prime}
on
X
determined by the metrics
g
and
\hat{g}
, respectively, under
theorem

2.3.1
coincide, i.e.,
g^{\prime}=\hat{g}^{\prime}
.

2.

The families of inner products
h
and
\hat{h}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
determined by the metrics
g
and
\hat{g}
, respectively, under
theorem

2.3.1
, satisfy
h^{-1}=\hat{h}
under their identification with
(n\times n)
-matrices.

3.

Every point
x\in X
has an open neighborhood
U\subseteq M
such that the following structures exist:

(a)

Trivializations
\varphi:U\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to E|_{U}
and
\hat{\varphi}:U\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\hat{E}|_{U}
of principal
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-bundles over
U
.

(b)

Two 2-forms
B,\hat{B}\in\Omega^{2}(U\times\mathbb{T}^{n})
together with connection-preserving isomorphisms
\mathcal{T}:\varphi^{*}\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{I}_{B}
and
\hat{\mathcal{T}}:\hat{\varphi}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}\to\mathcal{I}_{\hat{B}}
over
U\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
.

(c)

Consider
U\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}
with projection maps
{\mathrm{pr}},\hat{\mathrm{pr}}
to
U\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. Further, consider the map
\Phi:U\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to E\times_{X}\hat{E}
defined by
\Phi(x,a,\hat{a}):=(\varphi(x,a),\hat{\varphi}(x,\hat{a}))
.
Let
P
denote the principal
\mathbb{T}
-bundle with connection over
U\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}
that corresponds to the isomorphism

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.27888pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.27888pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}B}={\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.93161pt\raise 6.80057pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{T}^{-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.27893pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 65.27893pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\varphi^{*}\mathcal{G}=\Phi^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{G}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 57.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.59984pt\raise-21.43529pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Phi^{*}\mathcal{D}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.9834pt\raise-30.00005pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-30.50005pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise-30.50005pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 59.84354pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-59.84354pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Phi^{*}(\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\rho})=\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\varphi}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\Phi^{*}\rho}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.7324pt\raise 7.02779pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.19444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{T}}\otimes\mathrm{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.8436pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 99.8436pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\hat{B}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\Phi^{*}\rho}=\mathcal{I}_{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{B}+\Phi^{*}\rho}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

under the equivalence of
proposition

2.1.4
.
We require a connection-preserving isomorphism

P\cong{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}\text{,}

where
\mathcal{P}
is the
n
-fold Poincaré bundle with its canonical connection.

The set of equivalence classes of geometric T-duality correspondences over
X
(with the equivalence relation just as in
definition

4.1.5
) is denoted by
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.

Remark 4.1.10
.

If
\mathcal{D}
is a geometric T-duality correspondence, then the inverse correspondence
s^{*}\mathcal{D}^{-1}
of
remark

4.1.4
is also a geometric T-duality correspondence.
Indeed, conditions
and
are obviously symmetric, and in
it is straightforward to show that swapping
s
and inversion
\mathcal{D}^{-1}
have
both
the effect of dualizing the bundle
P
.
Thus, geometric T-duality is a symmetric relation on the set
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.

Remark 4.1.11
.

It is straightforward to see that the action of
remark

4.1.7
restricts to an action of
\hat{\mathrm{H}}^{3}(X)
on
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
. The properties of this action are best studied in the context of differential T-duality and carried out in differential cohomology, see
proposition

6.2.6
. The result of
proposition

6.2.6
is the following.

Proposition 4.1.12
.

Let

\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)\to\mathrm{Bun}^{\nabla}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}(X)\times\mathrm{Bun}^{\nabla}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}(X)

(4.1.5)

be the projection to the isomorphism classes of the principal
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-bundles
E
and
\hat{E}
and their Kaluza-Klein connections
\omega
and
\hat{\omega}
induced by the metric
g
and
\hat{g}
, respectively. We denote by
(F,\hat{F})\in\Omega^{2}(X)\times\Omega^{2}(X)
the well-defined pair of curvature forms. Consider the subgroup

\mathcal{F}_{F,\hat{F}}:=\{\mathcal{I}_{\hat{y}F+y\hat{F}}\;|\;y,\hat{y}\in\mathbb{R}\}\subseteq\mathrm{Grb}^{\nabla}(X)\text{.}

Then, the quotient
\mathrm{Grb}^{\nabla}(X)/\mathcal{F}_{F,\hat{F}}
acts free and transitively in the fibre of
eq.

4.1.5
over an element with curvature pair
(F,\hat{F})
.

Remark 4.1.13
.

The assignments
X\mapsto\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{BG}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
and
X\mapsto\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
are presheaves on the category of smooth manifolds. In fact, it is straightforward and only omitted for brevity to enhance the sets
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{BG}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
and
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
to bicategories, which then form
sheaves
of bicategories on the site of smooth manifolds.

4.2
Relation to Buscher rules

We will now make a deeper analysis of condition
, and in particular show that the Buscher rules are satisfied over
U
.

Proposition 4.2.1
.

Let
((E,g,\mathcal{G}),(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}}),\mathcal{D})
be a geometric T-duality correspondence. Consider an open set
U\subseteq X
together with the structure listed in
. Then, the
Buscher pairs
(g,B)
and
(\hat{g},\hat{B})
satisfy the Buscher rules.

Applying
proposition

2.1.4
to the bundle gerbe isomorphism in
yields

\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{B}-{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}B={\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\Omega-\Phi^{*}\rho_{g,\hat{g}}\text{.}

In addition to
and
,
lemmas

3.3.1
and
3.2.2
show that
(g,B)
and
(\hat{g},\hat{B})
satisfy the Buscher rules.
∎

Conversely, geometric T-duality locally does not pose any more conditions than the Buscher rules. To see this, we observe that any Buscher pair
(g,B)
extends to a geometric T-duality background, with
E^{s,n}:=\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
, the given metric
g
, and the trivial bundle gerbe
\mathcal{I}_{B}
.
If Buscher pairs
(g,B)
and
(\hat{g},\hat{B})
satisfy the Buscher rules, then we have
\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{B}-{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}B+\rho_{g,\hat{g}}={\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\Omega
by
lemma

3.3.1
. Thus,
{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}
corresponds under
proposition

2.1.4
to a connection-preserving isomorphism

\mathcal{D}:{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{B}\to\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{\hat{B}}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{\rho_{g,\hat{g}}}

(4.2.1)

over the correspondence space

E^{s,n}\times_{\mathbb{R}^{s}}E^{s,n}
.

Proposition 4.2.2
.

Suppose
(g,B)
and
(\hat{g},\hat{B})
are Buscher pairs and satisfy the Buscher rules. Then, the connection-preserving isomorphism
eq.

4.2.1
establishes a geometric T-duality correspondence between
(E^{s,n},g,\mathcal{I}_{B})
and
(E^{s,n},\hat{g},\mathcal{I}_{\hat{B}})
.

Conditions
and
of
definition

4.1.9
are
lemma

3.3.1
(a) and (b). That condition

is satisfied can be seen using the identity trivializations
\varphi,\hat{\varphi}
and
\mathcal{T},\hat{\mathcal{T}}
.
∎

4.3
Relation to topological T-duality

We shall first recall the definition of topological T-duality following
[
BS05
,
BRS06
,
MR06a
]
.

Definition 4.3.1
.

A
topological T-background
over
X
is a principal
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-bundle
E
together with a bundle gerbe
\mathcal{G}
over
E
. Two topological T-backgrounds
(E,\mathcal{G})
and
(E^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
over
X
are
equivalent
if there exists a bundle isomorphism
\varphi:E\to E^{\prime}
and a bundle gerbe isomorphism
\mathcal{G}\cong\varphi^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Equivalence classes of topological T-backgrounds over
X
form a set
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{BG}^{\mathrm{top}}(X)
.

Every geometric T-background
(E,g,\mathcal{G})
induces a topological T-background
(E,\mathcal{G})
by forgetting the metric and forgetting the gerbe connection. Conversely, if
(E,\mathcal{G})
is a topological T-background, one can choose any
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant metric on
E
and use the fact that every bundle gerbe admits a connection
[
Mur96
]
, to upgrade it to a geometric T-background.
Thus, we have a surjective map

\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{BG}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)\to\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{BG}^{\mathrm{top}}(X)\text{.}

Definition 4.3.2
.

A
topological correspondence
between T-backgrounds
(E,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
is an isomorphism
\mathcal{D}:{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{G}\to\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}
over
E\times_{X}E
. A topological correspondence
\mathcal{D}
is called topological T-duality correspondence, if each point
x\in X
has an open neighborhood
U\subseteq X
such that the following structures exist:

1.

Trivializations
\varphi:U\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to E|_{U}
and
\hat{\varphi}:U\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\hat{E}|_{U}
of principal
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-bundles over
U
.

2.

Bundle gerbe isomorphisms
\mathcal{T}:\varphi^{*}\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{I}
and
\hat{\mathcal{T}}:\hat{\varphi}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}\to\mathcal{I}
over
U\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
.

3.

The bundle gerbe isomorphism

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.80562pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.80562pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\varphi^{*}\mathcal{G}=\Phi^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 168.15294pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 168.15294pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Phi^{*}(\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}})=\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\varphi}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 255.22943pt\raise 6.61111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\mathcal{T}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 285.87532pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 285.87532pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{I}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

over
U\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}
corresponds under the equivalence of
proposition

2.1.4
to
{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}
.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

If
\mathcal{D}
is a geometric T-duality correspondence between two geometric T-backgrounds

(E,g,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
over
X
, then
\mathcal{D}
is a topological T-duality correspondence between the topological T-backgrounds
(E,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
.

Discarding all metrics and connections from
definitions

4.1.2
and
4.1.9
results precisely in
definition

4.3.1
.
∎

Corollary 4.3.4
.

If two geometric T-backgrounds

(E,g,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
over
X
are in geometric T-duality correspondence, then the homomorphism

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 233.22171pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern of twisted K-theory groups is an isomorphism.

There is also an interesting converse question. Suppose two topological T-backgrounds are in topological T-duality correspondence. Can one lift them to geometric T-backgrounds that are in geometric T-duality correspondence?

Proposition 4.3.5
.

Every topological T-duality correspondence can be lifted to a geometric T-duality correspondence. In more detail,
suppose
(E,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
are topological T-backgrounds, and suppose
\mathcal{D}
is a topological T-duality correspondence. Then, there exist
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-equivariant metrics
g
and
\hat{g}
on
E
and
\hat{E}
, connections on
\mathcal{G}
and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
and a connection on
\mathcal{D}
such that
\mathcal{D}
is a geometric T-duality correspondence between
(E,g,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
.

Combines
propositions

6.1.5
and
6.1.4
, to be proved later using the local formalism.
∎

Remark 4.3.6
.

It is straightforward to see that
proposition

4.3.3

induces a map

\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)\to\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{top}}(X)\text{.}

proposition

4.3.5
implies that this map is surjective.

Remark 4.3.7
.

A purely topological version of the action of
remark

4.1.11
,

\mathrm{H}^{3}(X)\times\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{top}}(X)\to\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{top}}(X)

exists, and it obviously acts in the fibres of the map
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{top}}(X)\to\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}(X)\times\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}(X)
. Bunke-Rumpf-Schick have investigated a similar action in
[
BRS06
, §7.2]
.

4.4
Relation to T-duality with H-flux

In this section we show that geometric T-duality implies T-duality with H-flux in the sense developed by Bouwknegt-Evslin-Mathai in
[
BEM04b
]
and Bouwknegt-Hannabuss-Mathai in
[
BHM04
]
. In these papers, T-duality is not considered as a relation between T-backgrounds, but rather as a transformation that takes a T-background to another.
A description of T-duality with H-flux as a relation on a class of suitable backgrounds has been given by Gualtieri-Cavalcanti in
[
CG10
]
based on
[
BEM04b
,
BHM04
]
, and we will use this here.

Definition 4.4.1
.

A
T-background with H-flux
over
X
is a principal
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-bundle
E
over
X
together with a closed 3-form
H\in\Omega^{3}(E)
with integral periods.

Every geometric T-background
(E,g,\mathcal{G})
induces one with H-flux where the metric
g
is forgotten and
H
is the curvature of
\mathcal{G}
.
Conversely, every T-background with H-flux
(E,H)
can be upgraded to a geometric T-background by choosing some
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant metric and some bundle gerbe with connection of curvature
H
.

For the following definition, we consider the correspondence space
E\times_{X}\hat{E}
, and at each point
(e,\hat{e})
, projecting to some
x\in X
, the subspaces
V_{e},\hat{V}_{\hat{e}}\subseteq T_{e,\hat{e}}(E\times_{X}\hat{E})=T_{e}E\times_{T_{x}X}T_{\hat{e}}\hat{E}
obtained as the image of the maps

\displaystyle i:\mathbb{R}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}V_{e}E\hookrightarrow T_{e}E\times_{T_{x}X}T_{\hat{e}}\hat{E},\quad v\mapsto(T_{1}R_{e}(v),0)

\displaystyle V_{e}:=\mathrm{Im}(i)

\displaystyle\hat{i}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\hat{V}_{\hat{e}}E\hookrightarrow T_{e}E\times_{T_{x}X}T_{\hat{e}}\hat{E},\quad w\mapsto(0,T_{1}R_{\hat{e}}(w))

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{\hat{e}}:=\mathrm{Im}(\hat{i})\text{.}

Definition 4.4.2
.

A
T-duality correspondence with H-flux
consists of two T-backgrounds with H-flux
(E,H)
and
(\hat{E},\hat{H})
and a
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
-invariant 2-form
F\in\Omega^{2}(E\times_{X}\hat{E})
such that

1.

{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}H-\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{H}=\mathrm{d}F
.

2.

The restriction of
F_{e,\hat{e}}
to
V_{e}\times\hat{V}_{\hat{e}}
is non-degenerate, for all
(e,\hat{e})\in E\times_{X}\hat{E}
.

Now we are in position to show that geometric T-duality reduces to T-duality with H-flux. This is a result of Kunath’s PhD thesis
[
Kun21
, Thm. 5.10]
.

Proposition 4.4.3
.

Suppose
(E,g,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
are in geometric T-duality correspondence
\mathcal{D}
. Then,
F:=\rho_{g,\hat{g}}
defined in
eq.

4.1.1
is a T-duality correspondence with H-flux.

As remarked in
section

4.1
, the 2-form
\rho_{g,\hat{g}}
is
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
-invariant.
The first condition is proved in
remark

4.1.3
. For the second condition, we have

\displaystyle F_{e,\hat{e}}((T_{1}R_{e}(v),0),(0,T_{1}R_{\hat{e}}(w)))

\displaystyle=\hat{\omega}_{\hat{e}}(0)\cdot\omega_{e}(0)-\hat{\omega}_{\hat{e}}(T_{1}R_{\hat{e}}(w))\cdot\omega_{e}(T_{1}R_{e}(v))=-w\cdot v\text{;}

i.e., we obtain (minus) the standard scalar product of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, which is non-degenerate.
∎

Remark 4.4.4
.

For a general base manifold
X
, one cannot expect that every given T-duality correspondence with
H-flux can be upgraded to a geometric (or only topological) T-duality correspondence. Indeed, a topological T-duality correspondence implies the triviality of the class
c_{1}(E)\cup c_{1}(\hat{E})\in\mathrm{H}^{4}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, while a T-duality correspondence with H-flux only implies the triviality of that class in de Rham cohomology.

5
Local perspective to geometric T-duality

We may see condition
of
definition

4.1.9
as enforcing a geometric T-duality correspondence to be
locally trivial
. Just as for locally trivial fibre bundles, one may then extract “local data”, or “gluing data”. It is instructive to first do this in an ad hoc manner, which is the content of
section

5.1
. In
section

5.2
we organize local data in a more systematic way, establishing the table in
fig.

1.2
of
section

1
.
sections

5.3
,
5.4
and
5.5

are devoted to a full proof of a bijection between the set
\mathrm{T}\text{-}\mathrm{Corr}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
of equivalence classes of geometric T-duality correspondences and a set
\mathrm{Loc}^{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
of equivalence classes of local data. In
section

5.6
we reduce the discussion of local data to
topological
T-duality, and show that this reduction becomes the non-abelian cohomology with values in the T-duality 2-group.

5.1
Extraction of local data

We suppose that we have a geometric T-duality correspondence
\mathcal{D}
as in
definition

4.1.9
, between geometric T-backgrounds
(E,g,\mathcal{G})
and
(\hat{E},\hat{g},\hat{\mathcal{G}})
over
X
.
We assume then that
X
is covered by open sets
U_{i}
over which condition
holds, and that corresponding bundle trivializations
\varphi_{i},\hat{\varphi}_{i}
, bundle gerbe trivializations
\mathcal{T}_{i},\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
and 2-isomorphisms
\xi_{i}
are chosen for all
U_{i}
, where
\xi_{i}
are the connection-preserving 2-isomorphisms

\xi_{i}:\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{i}\circ\Phi_{i}^{*}\mathcal{D}\circ\mathcal{T}_{i}^{-1}\Rightarrow{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}\text{.}

(5.1.1)

Let
a_{ij}:U_{i}\cap U_{j}\to\mathbb{T}^{n}
be the transition functions of
E
, which are determined by the trivializations
\varphi_{i}
and
\varphi_{j}
, i.e.,
\varphi_{j}(x,a)\cdot a_{ij}(x)=\varphi_{i}(x,a)
.
It will soon become necessary to choose and fix lifts of these transition functions along
\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}^{n}
, which is always possible after eventually passing to a refinement of the open cover. The former cocycle condition then reveals “winding numbers”
m_{ijk}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle a_{ij}+a_{jk}+m_{ijk}

\displaystyle=a_{ik}

(5.1.2)

and these integers
m_{ijk}
themselves satisfy the usual Čech cocycle condition. We will also denote by
a_{ij}
the corresponding map

(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}:(x,a)\mapsto(x,a+a_{ij}(x))

that multiplies by
a_{ij}(x)
; note that this map
satisfies
\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{j}\circ a_{ij}
.
Next, we consider the composite

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.83098pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.83098pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{B_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.84483pt\raise 6.62001pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.62001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{T}_{i}^{-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.83102pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.83102pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\varphi_{i}^{*}\mathcal{G}=a_{ij}^{*}\varphi_{j}^{*}\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 108.59477pt\raise 6.6582pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.68599pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{ij}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{j}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 136.68758pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 136.68758pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{a_{ij}^{*}B_{j}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

of bundle gerbe isomorphisms over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
,
which corresponds by
proposition

2.1.4
to a principal
\mathbb{T}
-bundle
L_{ij}
over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
with connection of curvature
a_{ij}^{*}B_{j}-B_{i}
.

The same works on the dual side, resulting in transition functions
\hat{a}_{ij}:U_{i}\cap U_{j}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, winding numbers
\hat{m}_{ijk}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
satisfying

\displaystyle\hat{a}_{ij}+\hat{a}_{jk}+\hat{m}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=\hat{a}_{ik}\text{,}

(5.1.3)

and principal
\mathbb{T}
-bundles
\hat{L}_{ij}
over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
with connection of curvature
\hat{a}_{ij}^{*}\hat{B}_{j}-\hat{B}_{i}
.

Before we proceed, we remark that the local trivializations
\varphi_{i},\hat{\varphi}_{i}
also define local
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant metrics
g_{i}:=\varphi_{i}^{*}g
and
\hat{g}_{i}:=\hat{\varphi}_{i}^{*}\hat{g}
on
U_{i}\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. Due to the
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariance of
g
and
\hat{g}
, we have

a_{ij}^{*}g_{j}=g_{i}\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{a}_{ij}^{*}\hat{g}_{j}=\hat{g}_{i}\text{.}

(5.1.4)

As seen in
proposition

4.2.1
, the pairs
(g_{i},B_{i})
and
(\hat{g}_{i},\hat{B}_{i})
satisfy the Buscher rules.

Lemma 5.1.1
.

The principal
\mathbb{T}
-bundles
L_{ij}
and
\hat{L}_{ij}
are trivializable. Thus, there exist
A_{ij},\hat{A}_{ij}\in\Omega^{1}((U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n})
and connection-preserving isomorphisms

\lambda_{ij}:L_{ij}\to\mathbf{I}_{A_{ij}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{\lambda}_{ij}:\hat{L}_{ij}\to\mathbf{I}_{\hat{A}_{ij}}

over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. In particular,
we have

a_{ij}^{*}B_{j}-B_{i}=\mathrm{d}A_{ij}\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{a}_{ij}^{*}\hat{B}_{j}-\hat{B}_{i}=\mathrm{d}\hat{A}_{ij}\text{.}

(5.1.5)

We assume that all non-empty double intersections
U_{i}\cap U_{j}
are contractible; this can again be achieved by passing to a refinement.
Then, the first Chern classes of
L_{ij}
and
\hat{L}_{ij}
must be pullbacks from
\mathbb{T}^{n}
. We have
\mathrm{H}^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{n},\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathfrak{so}(n,\mathbb{Z})
, the group of skew-symmetric integral
(n\times n)
-matrices, and this isomorphism can be realized explicitly using the Poincaré bundle
\mathcal{P}
over
\mathbb{T}^{2}
: we send a matrix
D\in\mathfrak{so}(n,\mathbb{Z})
to the principal
\mathbb{T}
-bundle

\mathcal{P}_{D}:=\bigotimes_{1\leq\alpha<\beta\leq n}{\mathrm{pr}}_{\alpha\beta}^{*}\mathcal{P}^{D_{\alpha\beta}}\text{,}

see
[
NW20
, §B]
.
Thus, there exist unique matrices
D_{ij},\hat{D}_{ij}\in\mathfrak{so}(n,\mathbb{Z})
and (non-unique) bundle isomorphisms
L_{ij}\cong{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{D_{ij}}
and
\hat{L}_{ij}\cong{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\hat{D}_{ij}}
.
Taking connections into account, there exist 1-forms
A_{ij},\hat{A}_{ij}\in\Omega^{1}((U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n})
and connection-preserving isomorphisms

\lambda_{ij}:L_{ij}\to{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{D_{ij}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{A_{ij}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{\lambda}_{ij}:\hat{L}_{ij}\to{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\hat{D}_{ij}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{\hat{A}_{ij}}

over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
.

We show next that
D_{ij}=\hat{D}_{ij}=0
, implying the claim of the lemma. This will be a consequence of the geometric T-duality correspondence, and so we need to work over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\ \mathbb{T}^{2n}
. We consider the following maps:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{pr}}

\displaystyle:(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}:(x,a,\hat{a})\mapsto(x,a)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{pr}}

\displaystyle:(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}:(x,a,\hat{a})\mapsto(x,\hat{a})

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{ij}

\displaystyle:(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}:(x,a,\hat{a})\mapsto(x,a+a_{ij}(x),\hat{a}+\hat{a}_{ij}(x))

\displaystyle\Phi_{i}

\displaystyle:U_{i}\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to E\times_{X}\hat{E}:(x,a,\hat{a})\mapsto(\varphi_{i}(x,a),\hat{\varphi}_{i}(x,\hat{a}))\text{,}

and construct with them the following diagram of bundle gerbes with connections and connection-preserving isomorphisms over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 272.64542pt\raise-86.6655pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.94878pt\raise-0.31595pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.94878pt\raise 0.31595pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.44571pt\raise-95.23474pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.37502pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{\lambda}_{ij}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.94878pt\raise-0.31595pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.94878pt\raise 0.31595pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.94878pt\raise-0.31595pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.94878pt\raise 0.31595pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 233.45023pt\raise-63.98395pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.37502pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{a}_{ij}^{*}\xi_{j}^{-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 189.33492pt\raise-56.13673pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.99689pt\raise-0.07886pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.99689pt\raise 0.07886pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 110.90501pt\raise-11.72034pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.13391pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\lambda_{ij}^{-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 138.03871pt\raise-34.35966pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.99689pt\raise-0.07886pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.99689pt\raise 0.07886pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

(5.1.6)

The unlabelled double arrows are the canonical unit and counit 2-isomorphisms of the adjunction between a 1-isomorphism and its inverse.
The rectangular subdiagram in the middle commutes on the nose. The outer shape of the diagram is, via
proposition

2.1.4
, a connection-preserving bundle isomorphism

\eta_{ij}:\tilde{a}_{ij}^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}\mathcal{P}\otimes{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{D_{ij}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}A_{ij}}\cong\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\hat{D}_{ij}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{A}_{ij}}\otimes{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}\mathcal{P}

(5.1.7)

over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}
.

We shall forget the connections (and thus all trivial bundles) for a moment. Due to the equivariance of the Poincaré bundle discussed in
section

2.2
, the lifts
a_{ij}
and
\hat{a}_{ij}
determine an isomorphism
\tilde{a}_{ij}^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}\mathcal{P}\cong{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}
.
Using this in
eq.

5.1.7
, we are in the situation that all bundles are pulled back along the projection
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to\mathbb{T}^{2n}
. Hence, these bundles must already have been isomorphic before pullback; and we conclude that there exists a bundle isomorphism

\mathcal{P}\otimes{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{D_{ij}}\cong\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\hat{D}_{ij}}\otimes\mathcal{P}\text{.}

over
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
. Hence, there also exists a bundle isomorphism

{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{D_{ij}}\cong\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\hat{D}_{ij}}\text{,}

and this shows that both bundles separately are trivializable. This implies
D_{ij}=\hat{D}_{ij}=0
.
∎

Remark 5.1.2
.

The principal
\mathbb{T}
-bundle
L_{ij}
and
\hat{L}_{ij}
can be regarded as part of the gluing data for the bundle gerbes
\mathcal{G}
and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
, respectively. Their triviality in case of geometric (or only topological) T-duality shows that T-backgrounds that can be part of a T-duality correspondence are of a special kind. More precisely, it means exactly that the Dixmier-Douady classes of
\mathcal{G}
and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}
are in the second step of the filtration of
\mathrm{H}^{3}(E,\mathbb{Z})
that comes from the Serre spectral sequence, see
[
BRS06
]
and
[
NW20
, §2.1]
.

Next we will spend some time on finding trivializations
\lambda_{ij}
and
\hat{\lambda}_{ij}
with particular covariant derivatives
A_{ij}
and
\hat{A}_{ij}
.
We start with arbitrary choices as they exist by
lemma

5.1.1
and will then perform three revisions of the isomorphisms
\lambda
and
\hat{\lambda}
, and accordingly shift the 1-forms
A_{ij}
and
\hat{A}_{ij}
, finally arriving at
eq.

5.1.18
.

We will only discuss
A_{ij}
, the treatment of
\hat{A}_{ij}
is analogous.
We remark that due to
lemma

3.3.2
,
eq.

3.3.1
, the 2-form
a_{ij}^{*}B_{j}-B_{i}
is
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-invariant; moreover, we have

(a_{ij}^{*}B_{j}-B_{i})_{1+2}=(a_{ij}^{*}B_{j}-B_{i})_{1}-\hat{a}_{ij}^{*}\theta\dot{\wedge}\theta_{2}

(5.1.8)

over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}
.
Here, we use the notation introduced in
section

2.2
: an index
(..)_{\alpha}
means a pullback from the
\alpha
-th
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-factor, and the index
(..)_{1+2}
means a pullback along the addition of two
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-factors.

eqs.

5.1.5
and
5.1.8
imply

\mathrm{d}((A_{ij})_{2}-(A_{ij})_{1})=-\mathrm{d}(\hat{a}_{ij}\theta_{2-1})\text{.}

This shows that we have a closed 1-form

\displaystyle\alpha_{ij}

\displaystyle:=(A_{ij})_{2}-(A_{ij})_{1}+\hat{a}_{ij}\theta_{2-1}\in\Omega^{1}_{cl}((U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n})\text{.}

(5.1.9)

Since the de Rham cohomology class of
\alpha_{ij}
can only have contributions from the torus, and these contributions must be linear combinations of the generators
[\theta]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(S^{1},\mathbb{R})
, there exists a smooth map
\beta_{ij}:(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to\mathbb{R}
and vectors
p_{ij},q_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\alpha_{ij}=\mathrm{d}\beta_{ij}+p_{ij}\theta_{1}+q_{ij}\theta_{2}\text{.}

(5.1.10)

Moreover, since the definition of
\alpha_{ij}
is skew-symmetric with respect to the exchange of the two
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-factors; we have
q_{ij}=-p_{ij}
.
We may now shift the isomorphism
\lambda_{ij}
by the smooth map

(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}:(x,a)\mapsto-p_{ij}a\text{.}

Its derivative is
-p_{ij}\theta
; thus,
A_{ij}
becomes replaced by
A_{ij}+p_{ij}\theta
, and
eq.

5.1.10
is replaced by just

\displaystyle\alpha_{ij}=\mathrm{d}\beta_{ij}\text{.}

(5.1.11)

In particular, we have shown that
\lambda_{ij}
can be chosen such that
\alpha_{ij}
is trivial in de Rham cohomology.
The left hand side is still skew-symmetric, and so we have

\mathrm{d}(\beta_{ij}+s^{*}\beta_{ij})=0
,
where
s
is the map that swaps the
\mathbb{T}^{n}
factors.
This means that
c_{ij}:=\beta_{ij}(x,a,b)+\beta_{ij}(x,b,a)
is a constant function. Shifting
\beta_{ij}
by
-\frac{1}{2}c_{ij}
, we can achieve that
c_{ij}=0
, i.e., achieve that
\beta_{ij}
is skew-symmetric in
a
and
b
.

Over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{3n}
one can deduce from
eq.

5.1.9
the cocycle condition

(\mathrm{d}\beta_{ij})_{1,3}=(\mathrm{d}\beta_{ij})_{1,2}+(\mathrm{d}\beta_{ij})_{2,3}\text{.}

This shows that there exists a constant
c_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\beta_{ij}(x,a,c)=\beta_{ij}(x,b,c)+\beta_{ij}(x,a,b)+c_{ij}

for all
a,b,c\in\mathbb{T}^{n}
.
Putting
a=b=c
shows that
c_{ij}=0
. Putting
b=0
implies that

\beta_{ij}(x,a,c)=\beta_{ij}(x,0,c)+\beta_{ij}(x,a,0)\text{.}

Thus, we may define
\tilde{\beta}_{ij}:(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\tilde{\beta}_{ij}(x,a):=\beta_{ij}(x,a,0)
and obtain, using the skew-symmetry of
\beta_{ij}
,

\beta_{ij}(x,a,b)=\tilde{\beta}_{ij}(x,a)-\tilde{\beta}_{ij}(x,b)\text{.}

We are now in position to make a second revision of the choice of the isomorphism
\lambda_{ij}
, and shift it by the smooth map
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}:(x,a)\mapsto-\tilde{\beta}_{ij}(x,a)
. This shifts
A_{ij}
by
\mathrm{d}\tilde{\beta}_{ij}
.
Then,
eq.

5.1.11
is replaced by
\alpha_{ij}=0
, and
eq.

5.1.9
results in

\displaystyle(A_{ij})_{2}-(A_{ij})_{1}

\displaystyle=-\hat{a}_{ij}\theta_{2-1}\text{.}

(5.1.12)

On the dual side, we obtain analogously

\displaystyle(\hat{A}_{ij})_{2}-(\hat{A}_{ij})_{1}

\displaystyle=-a_{ij}\theta_{2-1}\text{.}

(5.1.13)

Next we have to bring
A_{ij}
and
\hat{A}_{ij}
together, and consider for this purpose the connection-preserving isomorphism
\eta_{ij}
of
eq.

5.1.7
.
By
lemma

5.1.1
, it simplifies to a connection-preserving isomorphism

\eta_{ij}:\tilde{a}_{ij}^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}A_{ij}}\to\mathbf{I}_{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{A}_{ij}}\otimes{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}\mathcal{P}\text{.}

(5.1.14)

As a result of the fixed lifts
a_{ij}
and
\hat{a}_{ij}
, we obtain canonically a connection-preserving isomorphism

R_{ij}:\tilde{a}_{ij}^{*}{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}\to{\mathrm{pr}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2n}}^{*}\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{\psi_{ij}}

(5.1.15)

over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}
,
see
eq.

2.2.6
.
Here,
\psi_{ij}\in\Omega^{1}((U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n})
with

\psi_{ij}:=-a_{ij}\mathrm{d}\hat{a}_{ij}-a_{ij}\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\theta+\hat{a}_{ij}{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\theta\text{,}

(5.1.16)

Under the isomorphism
R_{ij}
of
eq.

5.1.15
we obtain from
eq.

5.1.14
a connection-preserving bundle isomorphism

\mathbf{I}_{\psi_{ij}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}A_{ij}}\cong\mathbf{I}_{\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{A}_{ij}}\text{,}

which in turn corresponds via the bijection
eq.

2.2
to a smooth map
h_{ij}:(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to\mathbb{T}
such that

\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{A}_{ij}={\mathrm{pr}}^{*}A_{ij}+\psi_{ij}+h_{ij}^{*}\theta\text{.}

(5.1.17)

Lemma 5.1.3
.

The maps
h_{ij}
are independent of the
\mathbb{T}^{2n}
-factor.

Considering
eq.

5.1.17
over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j})\times\mathbb{T}^{4n}
twice,

\displaystyle(\hat{A}_{ij})_{4}

\displaystyle=(A_{ij})_{2}+(\psi_{ij})_{2,4}+(h_{ij}^{*}\theta)_{2,4}

\displaystyle(\hat{A}_{ij})_{3}

\displaystyle=(A_{ij})_{1}+(\psi_{ij})_{1,3}+(h_{ij}^{*}\theta)_{1,3}\text{,}

taking their difference, and using

eqs.

5.1.12
,
5.1.13
and
5.1.16
yields

(h_{ij}^{*}\theta)_{2,4}=(h_{ij}^{*}\theta)_{1,3}\text{.}

This implies that

z_{ij}:=h_{ij}(x,b,\hat{b})^{-1}\cdot h_{ij}(x,a,\hat{a})\in\mathbb{T}

is a constant. Putting
a=b
and
\hat{a}=\hat{b}
shows that
z_{ij}=0
. We obtain
h_{ij}(x,b,\hat{b})=h_{ij}(x,a,\hat{a})
.
This shows the claim.
∎

We now make one last revision of the choice of the isomorphism
\hat{\lambda}_{ij}
, and shift it by
h_{ij}
. This changes
\hat{A}_{ij}
by
h_{ij}^{*}\theta
, and hence turns
eq.

5.1.17
into

\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{A}_{ij}={\mathrm{pr}}^{*}A_{ij}+\psi_{ij}\text{.}

(5.1.18)

Note that
eqs.

5.1.12
and
5.1.13
continue to hold, as a change by a 1-form that does not depend on
\mathbb{T}^{n}
cancels itself on both sides.

The definition of the principal
\mathbb{T}
-bundle
L_{ij}
induces a canonical connection-preserving bundle isomorphism

L_{ij}\otimes a_{ij}^{*}L_{jk}\cong L_{ik}

over
(U_{i}\cap U_{j}\cap U_{k})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}
. Under the trivialization
\lambda_{ij}
, it corresponds to a smooth map

c_{ijk}:(U_{i}\cap U_{j}\cap U_{k})\times\mathbb{T}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}

such that

A_{ik}=A_{ij}+a_{ij}^{*}A_{jk}+c_{ijk}^{*}\theta.

(5.1.19)

Further, by going to a quadruple intersection, it is straightforward to see that we obtain a cocycle condition

a_{ij}^{*}c_{jkl}\cdot c_{ijl}=c_{ijk}\cdot c_{ikl}\text{.}

(5.1.20)

The same holds on the dual side, leading to a smooth map
\hat{c}_{ijk}
satisfying

\hat{A}_{ik}=\hat{A}_{ij}+\hat{a}_{ij}^{*}\hat{A}_{jk}+\hat{c}_{ijk}^{*}\theta

(5.1.21)

and the cocycle condition

a_{ij}^{*}\hat{c}_{jkl}\cdot\hat{c}_{ijl}=\hat{c}_{ijk}\cdot\hat{c}_{ikl}\text{.}

(5.1.22)

Lemma 5.1.4
.

The following equation of maps
(U_{i}\cap U_{j}\cap U_{k})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to\mathbb{T}
holds:

\hat{\mathrm{pr}}^{*}\hat{c}_{ijk}={\mathrm{pr}}^{*}c_{ijk}\cdot f_{ijk}\text{,}

where
f_{ijk}
is defined by the expression

f_{ijk}:(U_{i}\cap U_{j}\cap U_{k})\times\mathbb{T}^{2n}\to\mathbb{T}:(x,a,\hat{a})\mapsto\hat{m}_{ijk}a-m_{ijk}(\hat{a}+\hat{a}_{ik}(x))-a_{jk}(x)\hat{a}_{ij}(x)\text{.}

We put the diagrams
eq.

5.1.6
for
ij
and
jk
, respectively, next to each other. In the middle, two occurrences of
\tilde{a}_{jk}^{*}\xi_{j}
cancel, and we obtain the following equality of connection-preserving 2-isomorphisms:

(5.1.23)

Our choice of isomorphisms
L_{ij}\cong\mathbf{I}_{A_{ij}}
and
\hat{L}_{ij}\cong\mathbf{I}_{\hat{A}_{ij}}
is such that we have an equality
